Sell-side estimates

❌ D453 questions
C-6750C-6950C-7435

Group judgement written last by the Investment Analyst; shown first here

The sell side is thin (one visible broker on FactSet, two or three in total), aligned with management by construction, and forecasting a cautious margin recovery on flat revenue that is credible as a central case and conservative beyond FY27 on revenue. Its record on this company is small beats in normal years and one catastrophic miss when the customer's calendar moved; its estimates today are at a post-upgrade level with zero revision momentum, which is why the dashboard grades sell-side momentum D, and the share has de-rated 18% with the numbers unchanged. The gap between a cheap multiple and unrebased estimates is the central risk-reward fact for the next two reporting periods: a dated award confirms the numbers and the multiple has room to recover; a deferral cuts the numbers and the multiple has little further to fall. The Consumer's model should start from consensus FY27, add a modest premium on FY28 and FY29 revenue, correct capital expenditure upward, reconcile the depreciation basis, treat FY29 as a one-broker scenario, and carry an explicit deferral case with the FY25 template.

C-67

Sell-side forecasts for the next one to three years against the last one to five, line by line, and the credibility of the gap

C50

Question asked How do the sell-side's forecasts for the next one to three years compare with the last one to five years, for the main financial statement line items in particular and the granular working up to them, and how credible is the gap given the change forces and foundations found above?

The lines (consensus FY27, FY28, FY29 against company-basis history):

Revenue: £156m, £162m, £173m (Zeus £157.5m, £161.6m) against £85.0m, £127m, £163m, £125m, £154m for FY22 to FY26. Growth of 1.5%, 3.2%, 6.9% against a 16% compound; on the recurring £131m base FY27 is about +20% and FY28 about +3.5%. Credible for FY27 only if CAIN follow-ons, Power orders and restock deliver on time; conservative for FY28 and FY29 given the input forces.

Gross profit: £52.0m, £53.5m, giving 33.3% and 33.0% against 33.5% in FY26 and 31% to 32% in FY22 to FY25. Credible if memory inflation is passed through with a lag; the historical band says 32% to 34%.

EBITDA: £14.3m, £15.5m, £18.2m against £13.9m in FY26 on the brokers' basis (£20.1m FY24, £10.3m FY25); margins 9.1%, 9.6%, 10.5% against 9.0%. The one-year gap is small; the three-year step to 10.5% needs utilisation.

Depreciation and amortisation: £3.6m, £3.8m, £4.0m against FactSet's total of £6.1m in FY26, which includes about £1.76m of acquired-intangible amortisation and lease depreciation; the consensus line is on an adjusted basis and the Consumer should reconcile it rather than take the apparent fall.

EBIT: £9.8m, £10.7m, £13.0m, margins 6.3%, 6.6%, 7.5% against 6.2% (company basis) and a 9.2% FY21 to FY24 average. Credible as a slow path; it leaves the divisional targets undated and gives upside if Ashchurch fills.

Pre-tax profit: £8.8m, £9.7m, implying net interest of about £1.0m, consistent with net debt falling to about £2m then net cash; credible.

Tax and net income: £6.6m, £7.5m, £9.1m, implying a 25% rate against 24.1% adjusted in FY26; credible, with United States goodwill deductions a small cash-tax benefit.

EPS: 11.6p, 13.1p, 16.0p on about 57m shares (no dilution modelled; a 10% cap applies); growth 5%, 13%, 22% against 14.1p, about 13p, 20.0p, 6.2p, 11.0p. The FY29 figure is one broker's.

Cash flow: operating cash flow £11.8m, £12.5m, £14.3m; capital expenditure £2.8m, £2.8m, £1.7m against the company's "over £7.5m across FY27 and FY28" (the consensus is £2m to £3m light); free cash flow £5.7m, £6.5m, £9.7m; dividends 2.9p, 3.3p, 4.0p (payout 25% to 30%); debt £8.1m flat, equity £66.5m, £70.3m.

Working capital and other: not forecast by line; the consensus free cash flow implies modest absorption in FY27 and release later, consistent with the inventory commentary.

Credibility of the gap as a whole: the sell side forecasts a margin recovery on near-flat revenue, which is the operating-leverage mechanism of C-75 applied cautiously; it is credible as a central case, conservative on revenue beyond FY27 given the change forces found above, slightly light on capital expenditure, and silent on the deferral case that the company's own record says should carry a meaningful probability. The one-broker depth beyond FY28 means the Consumer should treat FY29 as a scenario, not a consensus.

C-69

Record of missing, meeting or beating guidance and estimates over one, three and five years, including in comparable conditions

C50

Question asked What is the company's record of missing, meeting or beating guidance and sell-side estimates over the last one, three and five years, including in comparable environmental conditions?

One year (FY26): beat at every line against both the original and the raised bar: revenue £154.1m against £145.0m original and £152.2m revised; adjusted pre-tax profit £8.6m against £7.2m and £7.9m (+8.8%); EPS 11.0p against 9.5p to 10.2p (+8%); net debt £4.2m against £4.3m; H1 FY26 revenue and profit both small beats against the November thresholds.

Three years (FY24 to FY26): FY24 met (record revenue £163.3m, 20.0p); FY25 missed catastrophically against original expectations (adjusted pre-tax profit £5.0m against £15.0m expected, a 60% EPS cut) for an external reason, then beat the rebased number (£5.0m against £4.2m); FY26 beat. Two beats, one miss, with the miss larger than both beats combined.

Five years (FY22 to FY26): FY22 and FY23 saw upgrades exceeding 20% in multiple periods as revenue grew 28% and 49%; FY24 met; FY25 missed; FY26 beat. The pattern is small beats in normal years and a large miss when the customer's calendar moved.

Comparable conditions: the comparable condition is a defence-procurement pause, and in that condition the record is a miss of 60%; in restocking or demand-recovery conditions (FY22, FY26) the record is beats of 5% to 20%. The current conditions combine both: a recovery in industrial demand and a delayed defence plan. The record therefore gives the Consumer a bimodal prior: a high probability of a modest beat on the broker number if awards arrive, and a meaningful probability of a large miss if they do not, with little in between.

C-74

Whether estimates have been cut deeply and fully across all analysts, and whether guidance has been rebased lower, kitchen-sinked or sandbagged

❌ D35

Question asked To what extent have sell-side estimates been cut deeply and fully across all analysts, and has company guidance recently been rebased lower, or more aggressively kitchen-sinked or sandbagged?

No. Estimates sit at a post-upgrade level and have not been cut; the share price has fallen without them.

Revisions: FactSet's consensus EPS for the company's FY28 (its "FY2027" row) is 13.1p, +1.0% over one and three months and -2.2% over six; for FY27 (its "FY2026" row) 11.6p, flat over one and three months and +10.1% over six; the FY29 one-broker figure is unchanged at 16.0p; sales revisions are 0%, 0%, -1%; the target price has risen 16% over six months to a 288p mean (Cavendish 301p; Zeus's fair value of 330p earlier); the buy share is 100% and unchanged; the dashboard's revision-momentum score is zero. The EBITDA revision of -20% over six months is inconsistent with Zeus's upgrade of FY27 EBITDA from £12.1m to £14.2m in June and with the FactSet EBITDA consensus of £14.3m to £15.5m, so it is most likely a contributor or definitional change rather than a cut, and the Consumer should verify it rather than use it.

Rebasing: the only rebasing was November 2024 (FY25 and FY26 EPS cut 60% and 46%), after which the numbers were rebuilt through 2025 and 2026; guidance has not been rebased lower since, the FY27 margin steer is "broadly stable" rather than a kitchen-sink, and the sandbagging is of the modest, habitual kind (a low bar beaten by 3% to 9%), not an aggressive reset.

Implication: the stock has de-rated (199p to 162.5p, about -18%, with the 24-month-forward P/E at the first percentile of five years) on timing fears while the estimates still carry the June upgrade; a deferral would therefore cut estimates from an unrebased level, which is the worst configuration for a holder, while a confirmation would re-rate the shares on unchanged numbers, which is the best. Estimates are not at trough; the multiple is.

Stock market narrative

C521 question
C-2252

Group judgement written last by the Investment Analyst; shown first here

The narrative the market can buy over the next six months is specific and dated: a follow-on award under Project CAIN, a Defence Investment Plan that names tactical communications, and a Capital Markets Day that puts numbers on the three-division bridge, into an interim result that will look worse than it is. If two of the three land, the story changes from "lumpy defence supplier that missed once" to "accredited sovereign platform filling built capacity", which is the sovereign-platform re-rating archetype, and the multiple has room from the first percentile. If none lands by December, the narrative reverts to the November 2024 template and the unrebased estimates become the problem. Beyond six months the staircase is real but undated, and its steps (NSPA follow-ons, United States antennas, Volklec, net cash) are the kind the market pays for only after the first has been proven. For the Consumer this group says: the eighteen-month model should be built around the two dated events and the interim phasing, and the four-year model around the programme pipeline, with explicit probabilities rather than a single path.

C-22

The staircase of novel positive developments ahead: next 6 months, months 6 to 18, months 18 to 36; novelty and foundation

C52

Question asked What staircase of novel positive developments lies ahead: which new, dated things can the market look forward to within the next 6 months, in months 6 to 18, and in months 18 to 36; how novel is each; and how well-founded?

Next six months (to April 2027): (1) A trading update ahead of the interim results, historically early November, with an order-book figure and any Project CAIN follow-on news; novel if it names a follow-on, well founded on the CAIN programme structure and the Earnings Review's own test (£15m of follow-ons by December 2026). (2) The Capital Markets Day in November 2026, the first under the new chief executive and chair and the first since the three-division structure; novel (the company has never given a numeric bridge, a utilisation figure or a programme-revenue share), well founded as a dated event, uncertain in content. (3) Publication of the UK Defence Investment Plan, which would convert committed spending into contract cadence; novel, dated only by government, strongly founded in policy. (4) The H1 FY27 results on 15 December 2026, which will show revenue down year on year on the comparator and will be judged on operating margin (above 5.5%), order book, Power growth and cash; not novel, and a risk as much as a step. (5) Further Power autonomy orders and Durakool data-centre orders, each announced as won; incremental rather than novel. (6) Research from Zeus as joint broker and from Berenberg, widening the coverage; modestly novel. (7) The April 2027 pre-close, the company's habitual upgrade point.

Months six to eighteen (to October 2028): (8) The FY27 results in June 2027 with the first evidence of Ashchurch utilisation and divisional margins against targets. (9) NSPA framework follow-on orders from non-UK NATO members, which Zeus calls the largest multi-year growth catalyst for Systems; novel and plausible, undated. (10) United States onshore antenna production starting and the first "Made in USA" antenna awards; novel, founded on the stated eighteen-month investment programme. (11) The Volklec sovereign battery collaboration producing a first land or naval electrification award; novel, undated. (12) Net cash by March 2028 on Zeus's model, opening the buyback or acquisition question. (13) A bolt-on antenna acquisition.

Months eighteen to thirty-six (to October 2029): (14) FY28 EPS against the LTIP band of 11.5p to 16.0p and the FY29 one-broker figure of 16.0p; the point at which the 2030 ambition of 20p is either in sight or not. (15) Group operating margin crossing 9%, the level the sell side names as the re-rating mechanism. (16) Power revenue approaching the £40m the Earnings Review sketches for FY29 as autonomy programmes scale. (17) A possible move from AIM to the Main Market, following Volex's precedent; not mooted. (18) Consolidation interest from a scaled acquirer, given category pricing.

Assessment: the staircase is front-loaded with three genuinely novel and dated items in the next six months (follow-on award, Capital Markets Day, Defence Investment Plan), none of which the company controls apart from the content of its own presentation, followed by a run of incremental and undated steps whose foundation is the strategy rather than a contract. The novelty is high; the foundation of the near-term steps is strong on policy and weak on timing, which is the whole case in one sentence.

Back to contents

The Investment Analyst's closing notes for this layer

Closing steer for the second set

The operating model should be built from programme timing and utilisation, not from a growth rate and a margin. Start from a recurring revenue base of about £131m in FY26 growing mid to high single digits, add the dated defence programmes (CAIN initial order delivered, follow-ons and NSPA as probability-weighted blocks), the Power autonomy order book and the components restock, and let the operating margin fall out of a largely fixed £42m overhead with a 25% to 30% drop-through on the variance. Carry a gross margin band of 32% to 34% for memory inflation and pass-through lag, working capital at 20% to 23% of revenue with a FY27 absorption and FY28 release, capital expenditure above consensus at about £3.5m to £4m a year, net interest of about £1m falling, a 24% to 25% tax rate, a flat 57m share count, no buyback and no acquisition in the base case. Hold three cases: consensus-like (FY27 EPS about 11.6p, FY28 13p, FY29 15p to 16p), an award-led case (FY28 15p to 16p, operating margin 8% to 9%), and the FY25 deferral template (EPS 6p to 8p). The questions the second set most needs answered are how much of the overhead is genuinely fixed and how fast Ashchurch converts revenue to profit, whether memory inflation is recovered in full and with what lag, and what the inputs below operating profit and the working-capital cycle do to free cash flow in each case; the research requests are written to those.
