Consensus for comparison (FactSet median, one broker): revenue £156m, £162m, £173m; EBITDA £14.3m, £15.5m, £18.2m; EBIT £9.8m, £10.7m, £13.0m; EPS 11.6p, 13.1p, 16.0p; free cash flow (FactSet definition) £5.7m, £6.5m, £9.7m. My base is 2% below consensus on FY27 revenue and 7% below on FY27 EPS; 2% above on FY28 revenue and 7% above on FY28 EPS; 3% above on FY29 revenue and 11% above on FY29 EPS.

The next 18 months by half-year. H1 FY27 (to 30 September 2026, reports 15 December): revenue about £75.5m (-12% on £85.7m), gross margin about 33.5%, adjusted operating profit about £4.2m (5.6% against 7.0%), EBITDA about £6.5m (8.6%), net debt modestly above March on inventory and the final dividend, order book around £100m. H2 FY27 (to 31 March 2027): revenue about £77.5m (+13% on £68.5m), operating profit about £5.0m (6.4%), EBITDA about £7.3m (9.4%), with the probability-weighted follow-on tranche inside it. H1 FY28 (to 30 September 2027): revenue about £81.5m (+8%), operating margin about 6.6%, EBITDA about £7.9m (9.7%). The interim print will show revenue down and the margin down year on year; the test is the margin above 5.5% and the order book above £95m with a programme component visible.

The three paths and their probabilities.

Base (55%): as above. Requires the Defence Investment Plan to be published by mid-2027, one CAIN follow-on tranche to ship in H2 FY27 or H1 FY28, NSPA follow-ons from FY28, Power's order book to convert, the restock to hold through FY27, memory inflation to be passed through with a lag, and overhead growth to be held to 4% to 5%.

Award-led (25%): a follow-on tranche of £9m to £12m ships in H2 FY27 and the plan names tactical communications and uncrewed-platform electronics; FY27 revenue £158m, margin 6.5%, EPS 11.9p; FY28 £178m, 8.3%, 17.5p; FY29 £196m, 9.8%, 22.5p; the 2030 ambition is met on time and net cash exceeds £10m by FY29, which brings the buyback or acquisition question forward.

Deferral (20%): the FY25 template; no tranche ships in FY27 and the plan slips into the second half of 2027; FY27 revenue £142m, margin 4.3%, EPS 7.0p; FY28 £156m, 6.0%, 11.0p (the LTIP threshold, not by coincidence); FY29 £170m, 7.5%, 15p. Working capital unwinds in FY27 so cash is not the problem; the multiple and the sell-side numbers are.

Probability-weighted EPS: about 10.3p FY27, 14.2p FY28, 18.1p FY29. The base path sits slightly above the weighted figure in FY27 and slightly below it in FY28 and FY29, because the deferral case is front-loaded and the award case is back-loaded; the Consumer should use the base path for the model and the weighted figures when judging consensus.

What would move me off the base case, in order of likelihood. (1) The November trading update or the interim naming a CAIN follow-on or an NSPA follow-on: move toward the award-led path. (2) The interim reporting an operating margin below 5.5% or an order book below £95m: move toward deferral. (3) The Capital Markets Day giving a dated margin bridge and an Ashchurch utilisation figure: raise FY29 to FY31 margins by half a point; giving a revenue-only reframing of 2030: lower them by the same. (4) A memory-driven programme deferral announced by the company: lower FY27 revenue by £5m to £10m and raise FY28 by the same. (5) A capital-allocation framework or buyback: raise FY29 to FY31 EPS by 5% to 10%. (6) A weak December print from discoverIE, TT Electronics or XP Power on the restock: lower Components growth by two points.

Where this base case differs from the first set's closing steer. The first set proposed three cases: consensus-like (FY27 about 11.6p, FY28 13p, FY29 15p to 16p), award-led (FY28 15p to 16p, margin 8% to 9%) and the FY25 template (6p to 8p). My base case is below its consensus-like case for FY27 (10.8p) and above it for FY28 and FY29 (14.0p and 17.7p), because I have applied the timing haircut to FY27 and the catch-up and utilisation to FY28 and FY29 rather than running a smooth slope; my award-led case is a year ahead of its own; and my deferral case is the same. I also carry higher capital spending than consensus (as the first set advised), a strict free-cash-flow definition, and no working-capital release in FY28.

Part 4. The reasoning behind the inputs

Segments: revenue and margin by segment, where the company reports them

The company reports three divisions (Systems, Power and Components) with revenue, gross margin and adjusted operating margin for each; Power was separated from Systems in FY26, so FY25 is the first comparable year. FactSet carries no segment forecasts. The base-case segment build behind the group inputs is below (adjusted operating margin before central costs and before amortisation of acquired intangibles).

Systems FY26 included the £23.3m NATO Support and Procurement Agency communications programme that does not repeat; its recurring base was about £39m. FY27 Systems is that base growing 4% to 6%, plus Project CAIN's £8.0m initial order (delivered in the quarter to June 2026), the DSTL cabinet contract and a probability-weighted follow-on tranche of about £5m in the second half (55% of about £9m). From FY28 Systems adds £15m to £20m a year of CAIN follow-ons, NATO framework follow-ons and Tier 2 sub-system work as the Ashchurch facility loads toward its £15m of headroom, which takes its margin to the top of the 12.5% to 15.0% target by FY29. Power grows 15% in FY27 on the over $20m of autonomy orders taken in the last four months of FY26 and $7.4m of Tier 1 wins, then 7% to 11% a year, reaching the bottom of its 10% to 14% margin target by FY30. Components grows 4.5% in FY27 (about one point of it pass-through inflation) on the industrial restock, AI data-centre contactors and own-brand lines, then 2% to 5.5%, with its margin rising on mix but staying below the 7% to 9% target. Gross margin by division: Systems about 38.5% to 39%, Power 36% rising to 37%, Components 27% rising to 27.5%; group 33.6% FY27 rising 20 basis points a year to 34.2% FY31 (mix and the exit of low-value work, net of memory inflation passed through at a lag).

The operating drivers behind the inputs

Revenue growth: a recurring base of about £131m (FY26 reported £154.1m less the £23.3m programme) growing 5% to 6% a year, plus a programme block of £13m in FY27 (CAIN initial order plus a weighted follow-on) rising to £20m to £30m a year from FY28 as UK and NATO defence budgets (rising 8% to 9% a year on Berenberg's December 2025 figures) convert into tranches once the Defence Investment Plan is published. The order book was £102.4m at 31 May 2026, £18m higher underlying after removing the £19m NSPA order from the prior-year figure, with most of it due to ship within eighteen months. The FY27 half-year phasing reflects the absence of the £23.3m programme from H1 (H1 FY26 revenue £85.7m), so H1 FY27 is modelled at about £75.5m (down 12%) and H2 FY27 at about £77.5m (up 13% on £68.5m).

EBITDA margin: adjusted operating profit plus depreciation of owned and right-of-use assets and amortisation of capitalised development (the brokers' adjusted basis; FY26 actual 9.0% on that basis). The margin path is the output of a largely fixed adjusted overhead (£42.0m in FY26, of which about £36m to £38m is fixed within a year) growing 4% to 5% a year (£42.2m FY27 including the £0.5m Autumn Budget employer National Insurance and wage effect, £44.4m FY28, £46.4m FY29, £48.7m FY30, £51.0m FY31) against revenue growing 6% to 8% from FY28, giving a net drop-through of 21% to 25% of incremental revenue to operating profit. The H1 FY27 EBITDA margin of 8.6% corresponds to an adjusted operating margin of about 5.6% (the thesis-at-risk level the first set set is 5.5%); H2 FY27 9.4% corresponds to about 6.4%; H1 FY28 9.7% to about 6.6%.

Depreciation and amortisation: FY26 total of £6.1m comprised £1.71m of depreciation of property, plant and equipment, £1.47m of right-of-use asset depreciation, and £2.90m of amortisation of which £1.76m was acquired intangibles (excluded here) and about £1.1m capitalised development and software; the £4.3m excluding acquired intangibles rises with the capital-spending step (over £7.5m across FY27 and FY28 per the company) to £4.6m, £5.0m, £5.3m, £5.5m, £5.7m.

Net financial items: £1.0m on the adjusted basis in FY26 (bank interest at 2.3% to 2.6% over SONIA, EURIBOR or SOFR on about £8m of average drawings, lease interest of about £0.3m, fees); falling as net debt of £4.2m (excluding £5.9m of leases) reaches about £3.7m at March 2027 and about nil at March 2028, then net cash of about £5.6m, £12m and £19m; lease interest and facility fees set a floor of £0.2m to £0.3m. Imputed interest on deferred consideration (£0.09m in FY26) is excluded as the brokers exclude it.

Tax rate: 24.1% adjusted effective rate in FY26 (management's "underlying" 26%; UK rate 25%; United States losses recognised); 25% assumed throughout.

Dividend per share: 2.75p for FY26 (raised 10%, covered 4.0x); 3.0p, 3.4p, 3.9p, 4.4p, 4.9p, a payout of 28% in FY27 falling to about 22% as earnings grow faster than the dividend, in line with the FY24 payout of about 21.5% at the last earnings peak. Consensus 2.9p, 3.3p, 4.0p.

Share count: 57.1m issued, 282,008 in treasury, FactSet diluted 57.6m; 1.27m options outstanding and up to 730,000 granted in May 2026 vesting between FY28 adjusted EPS of 11.5p and 16.0p; option exercises (158,500 in September 2026) add about 0.2m to 0.3m shares a year. No buyback is modelled; treasury purchases of about £0.2m a year for employee schemes are entered as the buyback line. No acquisitions are modelled; the balance sheet (£15.0m facility plus £10.0m accordion, net cash from FY28) could fund a bolt-on antenna acquisition or a buyback, which management has not signalled and which would be upside to EPS from FY29.

Capital spending: the company's "over £7.5m across FY27 and FY28" (the finance director, 30 June 2026, confirmed the figure is cumulative), for the Tewkesbury integrated systems facility, the near-field RF testing chamber, United States antenna production and Power's test rooms, including about £1.0m to £1.2m a year of capitalised development and software; then about £3.5m a year. Consensus carries £2.8m, £2.8m and £1.7m on the fixed-asset line only, which is light.

Working capital: net working capital (receivables plus inventories less payables) was £30.6m, 19.8% of revenue, at March 2026, within the 19% to 24% range of FY23 to FY26; FY27 rises to 21.5% on strategic memory and semiconductor stock (inventories £30.5m at March 2026 with more promised; work in progress £10.96m ahead of CAIN), easing to 20.5% in FY28 as CAIN work in progress ships and to 20% thereafter. Contract liabilities (£6.3m) and milestone billing moderate the swings; half-year balances move £4m to £7m around the year-end figure.

Lease payments: right-of-use depreciation of £1.47m plus lease interest of about £0.3m in FY26, so about £1.8m a year rising with the new sites.

The company's guidance, and how your inputs compare with it

The company issues no numeric guidance; it defines market expectations as the average of its covering brokers (Cavendish, Berenberg and Zeus Capital) and speaks to that average. Its qualitative steer at the FY26 results (29 June 2026) and in the annual report (6 July 2026) was: margins broadly stable in FY27 as investment continues ahead of revenue; capital expenditure over £7.5m across FY27 and FY28; further strategic inventory investment possible; delays to the UK Defence Investment Plan may affect the pace of order-book growth; component shortages may affect project timing and viability; the chief executive expressed high confidence of exceeding next-year market expectations. Medium-term markers: at least 20p of sustainable adjusted EPS by 2030, a £250m revenue milestone, divisional operating margin targets of 12.5% to 15.0% (Systems), 10.0% to 14.0% (Power) and 7.0% to 9.0% (Components); the May 2026 long-term incentive plan vests between FY28 adjusted EPS of 11.5p and 16.0p. Consensus (FactSet median at 22 October 2026, one visible broker): revenue £156m, £162m, £173m; EBITDA £14.3m, £15.5m, £18.2m; EBIT £9.8m, £10.7m, £13.0m; EPS 11.6p, 13.1p, 16.0p; Zeus (29 June 2026): revenue £157.5m and £161.6m, EPS 11.6p and 12.7p, operating margin 6.3% in FY27, net debt £2.1m at March 2027 and net cash £0.2m at March 2028.

My inputs sit below the company's "broadly stable" margin steer and below consensus for FY27 (operating margin 6.0% against 6.2% to 6.3%; revenue £153m against £156m to £157.5m; EPS 10.8p against 11.6p), because the second half of FY27 depends on a defence follow-on tranche whose timing the Defence Investment Plan delay has already pushed back once and I have probability-weighted it rather than assumed it. They sit above consensus for FY28 and FY29 (revenue £165.5m and £178.5m against £162m and £173m; EPS 14.0p and 17.7p against 13.1p and 16.0p), because consensus revenue growth of 3% and 7% is below the input forces and anything that slips out of FY27 lands in FY28, and because Ashchurch utilisation lifts the Systems margin faster than the brokers' slope. Against the company's 2030 ambition my base case reaches 20p of EPS a year late (19.8p in FY30, 21.8p in FY31) and reaches about £190m to £200m of revenue rather than £250m; it reaches the Systems margin target at the top of the range by FY29, the Power target at the bottom by FY30, and does not reach the Components target. The FY28 EPS of 14.0p sits between the LTIP midpoint (13.75p) and the ceiling (16.0p).

The bridge from the company's own reported figures to the basis consensus uses

Consensus and these inputs are on the company's adjusted basis, which the brokers adopt: adjusted operating profit is statutory operating profit before amortisation of acquired intangibles (£1.76m in FY26), reorganisation and deal costs (£0.68m in FY26; about £0.5m a year assumed, carried as a cash cost in the free-cash-flow cross-check) and share-based payment charges (about £0.4m); adjusted EBITDA adds back depreciation of owned and right-of-use assets and amortisation of capitalised development (£4.3m in FY26), so it is an IFRS 16 EBITDA and lease payments must be deducted in cash flow, as Part 2 does. FY26 on this basis: revenue £154.1m, gross margin 33.5%, adjusted operating profit £9.6m (6.2%), adjusted EBITDA £13.9m (9.0%), adjusted pre-tax profit £8.6m, adjusted diluted EPS 11.0p. Reported FY26: operating profit about £6.8m, pre-tax profit £5.8m (the adjusted-to-reported gap of £2.8m also includes £0.09m of imputed interest on deferred consideration), net income £4.1m, basic EPS 7.2p. FactSet's standardised history lines (gross margin 29.8%, operating EBITDA £13.5m, adjusted EBIT £7.5m, 4.8%) classify costs differently and should not be read against consensus; the financials block's "brokers' basis" actuals (revenue £154m, EBITDA £13.9m, EPS 11.0p) are the like-for-like base. Reported EPS in the base case is about 3.6p below adjusted EPS in FY27, narrowing to about 3.0p by FY31 as the acquired-intangible amortisation runs off (about £1.7m FY27 to £1.0m FY31). The FactSet revision table labels fiscal years one year behind the company's (its "FY2027" row of 13.1p is the company's FY28).

Your cross-check: your own earnings per share and free cash flow for each of the five fiscal years

Earnings per share is adjusted diluted EPS on the brokers' basis, in pounds as the financials block records it (0.108 is 10.8p; the pence figures are 10.8p, 14.0p, 17.7p, 19.8p and 21.8p). Free cash flow is on a strict definition: adjusted EBITDA less the working-capital movement, cash tax, cash interest, lease payments, all capital spending including intangibles, and reorganisation costs, before dividends, acquisitions and treasury purchases (FactSet's consensus free cash flow of £5.7m, £6.5m and £9.7m is before lease payments and intangible spending and is therefore about £2.5m to £3m a year higher on a like-for-like basis).

Appendix Research requests the Investment Analyst raised for Layer 3 and Layer 4

Research requests for the next set

Company: Solid State plc (SOLI-GB, AIM, London), a UK value-added electronics group supplying defence and security, industrial, medical and transport markets through three divisions: Systems (Steatite ruggedised computing and secure tactical radio as the exclusive UK partner for Persistent Systems' MPU5 radios and Wave Relay networks, Active Silicon imaging, Q-Par antennas in the UK and United States; the Ashchurch integrated systems facility opened in FY26), Power (Custom Power custom battery packs in the UK and California, separated as a division in FY26) and Components (the Solsta specialist distribution and own-brand business: Pacer, Willow Technologies, Gateway, Durakool); fiscal year to 31 March; FY26 revenue £154.1m including a £23.3m NATO Support and Procurement Agency communications programme delivered in H1 FY26; one customer 17% of FY26 revenue

Request: T-D1

Peers: discoverIE Group (DSCV-GB), the UK design-in electronics reference whose margin rose from about 3% to 13.8% over a decade through mix and acquisition, and which bought Trival Antene (defence antennas, a direct Q-Par competitor) and 3Gmetalworx in 2026; TT Electronics (TTG-GB), a UK defence and medical electronics maker that missed its 10% margin target for years before the Cleveland turnaround and Plano closure in 2025 to 2026, the precedent for a fixed-cost maker loading sites with defence volume; Volex (VLX-GB), whose operating margin went from 2.8% to 10.2% by exiting low-margin work and consolidating sites, and which beat its FY27 targets a year early; XP Power (XPP-GB), whose operating margin fell to 4.3% and recovered to 7.9% with factory utilisation across the 2024 to 2026 cycle; Gooch & Housego (GHH-GB), a UK defence photonics maker with second-half-weighted delivery and a recommended cash acquisition in July 2026; Huber+Suhner (HUBN-CH), whose Industry segment reported orders up 41.7% on defence; AQ Group (AQ-SE), whose defence bookings ran ahead of invoicing in 2025; the UK operations of Mercury Systems and General Micro Systems as private competitors for Ministry of Defence ruggedised computing; Persistent Systems' other international channel partners; UK and United States military battery-pack makers such as Lincad and Ultralife (ULBI-US) as Custom Power's competitors Operating conditions: the delayed publication of the UK Defence Investment Plan (still unpublished in October 2026) and the award cadence of Project CAIN follow-on tranches (initial $10.8m order announced 30 October 2025, delivered in the quarter to June 2026) and NATO Support and Procurement Agency framework follow-ons; a defence-procurement pause of the November 2024 kind, which cut the company's FY25 EPS expectations by about 60% (Zeus Capital, 15 November 2024); memory and semiconductor price inflation with extending lead times driven by AI data-centre demand, which the company said in June 2026 could make some programmes temporarily unviable;
