Sell-side estimates

1 question
I-0252
I-02

How likely are consensus forecasts, and the net breadth of revisions, to move higher or lower over the next 18 months, and why? Secondarily, the same for 18 months to three years out.

C52

Question asked How likely are sell-side consensus forecasts, and the net breadth of revisions, to move higher or lower over the next few earnings events (over the next 18 months): to be upgraded, cut or held, and why? Secondarily, how likely are the consensus forecasts for 18 months to three years out to be upgraded, cut or held?

The judgement. Consensus FY27 EPS (11.6p) is more likely to be cut than raised over the next six months and more likely to be raised than cut over months six to 18; consensus FY28 (12.7p to 13.1p) is more likely to be raised than cut over the whole period; and consensus FY29 (16.0p, one broker) is more likely to be introduced by the other houses at a higher figure than cut, provided the Capital Markets Day restates the ambition. The breadth of revisions, which barely exists today (three houses, one visible on FactSet, 100% Buy, a revision-momentum score of zero), will widen in both senses: Zeus's appointment as joint broker in October 2026 and Berenberg's coverage since December 2025 add contributors, and the Capital Markets Day is the point at which brokers introduce FY29 and FY30. The reason the direction differs by horizon is the sell side's own behaviour, which the record makes predictable: it revises at four points a year (the November update, the December interim, the April pre-close, the June results), it leaves forecasts unchanged on contract wins (the $10.8m CAIN order in October 2025 "underpinned" estimates) and on strong interims (December 2025), it cuts hard and across two years when a programme is deferred (November 2024: FY25 -60%, FY26 -46%), and it rebuilds in one step when the order is signed (March 2025: +20%). The base case's shape (a flat FY27, a staircase after) therefore produces a revision path of "hold or cut, then raise", and the deferral case produces "cut both years, then rebuild".

Where consensus sits, line by line, against the base case. Revenue: £156m, £162m, £173m against £153m, £165.5m, £178.5m (-2%, +2%, +3%). EBITDA: £14.3m, £15.5m, £18.2m against £13.8m, £16.6m, £19.7m (-4%, +7%, +8%). EBIT: £9.8m, £10.7m, £13.0m against £9.2m, £11.6m, £14.4m (-6%, +8%, +10%). EPS: 11.6p, 13.1p, 16.0p against 10.8p, 14.0p, 17.7p (-6%, +9%, +14%). Free cash flow (FactSet definition): £5.7m, £6.5m, £9.7m, which is about £2.5m to £3m a year above the strict definition and rests on capital expenditure of £2.8m, £2.8m, £1.7m against the company's "over £7.5m across FY27 and FY28"; the first revision to this line after the Capital Markets Day is likely to be down. Dividends 2.9p, 3.3p, 4.0p against 3.0p, 3.4p, 3.9p. The six-month EBITDA revision of -20% in the FactSet data is a definitional or contributor change (Zeus raised FY27 EBITDA from £12.1m to £14.2m in June) and should be disregarded; EPS revisions are +1%, +1%, -2% over one, three and six months, sales 0, 0, -1%, the target price +16% over six months to a 288p mean (Cavendish 301p).

The next six months: hold or cut. The November update will most likely leave consensus unchanged (Zeus held in November 2025; it cut in November 2024 only on a stated deferral). The Capital Markets Day will not move FY27 but may move FY28 and FY29 up if a bridge or a utilisation figure is given (the Volex precedent: +4% and +6% to EBITA within days), and may introduce FY29 at Zeus and Berenberg. The interim is the risk: an operating margin below 5.5% or a stated second-half slip produces cuts to FY27 of 10% to 30% and to FY28 of a similar order (the November 2024 pattern), and the probability of that outcome is about 35%. The probability that FY27 consensus is higher at the end of December than today is about 15% (a tranche named); unchanged about 50%; lower about 35%. The net breadth over six months is therefore more likely negative than positive, and the dashboard's "sell-side momentum D" is more likely to stay D than to improve before April.

Months six to 18: raise. The April 2027 pre-close and the June 2027 results are the company's and the brokers' upgrade points, and the base case's second half (£77.5m, +13%) with a tranche inside it gives the brokers what they need: in April 2026 Zeus lifted FY26 revenue by 3% and in June lifted FY27 EPS by 12.5%. If FY27 closes at or near 10.8p against an 11.6p consensus, the April statement will be "in line" or "slightly below" with FY28 held; if the tranche is larger than the weighted £5m, "ahead" with FY28 raised. In either path FY28 consensus moves toward the base case's 14.0p by June 2027 (probability about 55%), because the utilisation evidence from a shipped tranche is what the brokers have said they are waiting for. In the deferral path, FY27 and FY28 are cut in December or April and rebuilt only on signature, which could be any month; the March 2025 reinstatement came four months after the November 2024 cut.

Breadth, and how the widening coverage changes it. Today the "consensus" is one visible contributor and the company's three-house average; FactSet's median FY28 EPS of 13.1p differs from Cavendish's 12.7p, which means FactSet holds at least one more contributor it does not show. With Zeus as joint broker and Berenberg covering, the revision data will begin to behave like a series: a single house's cut will no longer be the whole consensus, and the net breadth measure (upgrades less downgrades over contributors) will start to carry information. Two consequences for the memo: the dashboard's revision-momentum score can move from zero to a positive reading only after April 2027 in the base case, and the memo's topic #8 (current momentum) should be written as "none, by construction" rather than "negative"; and because the brokers do not pre-empt orders, the first sign of a change in consensus will be the company's own words, not a broker note.

Secondarily, 18 months to three years. FY29 consensus (16.0p, Cavendish only) sits below the base case (17.7p) and at the top of the LTIP band; Zeus's FY29 will be introduced at the June 2027 results or earlier; Berenberg's is not visible. The probability that the three-house FY29 figure is above 16.0p by mid-2027 is about 55%, on Ashchurch loading and Power's margin path; the probability that it is below is about 25%, concentrated in the case where the Capital Markets Day reframes 2030 without a margin, which would take Cavendish's 7.5% FY29 margin down. FY30 consensus does not exist and will be introduced at or after the Capital Markets Day; the base case's 19.8p is the level at which it would need to sit for the 2030 ambition to be in sight, and the first FY30 numbers are likely to be published below it, in the brokers' habit.

Where our assessment sits against consensus. Below for FY27 (the sell side has not modelled the deferral case or the timing haircut, and has the year back-end loaded without saying so), above for FY28 and FY29 (the sell side models a slope on flat revenue and has not modelled the catch-up or the utilisation step). The gap is unfavourable for the next two events and favourable for the following four, and the consensus has the right direction with the wrong shape, which is the second set's conclusion restated from the sell side's own behaviour.

Back to contents

Stock market narrative

1 question
I-0358
I-03

How comprehensively are these dynamics articulated in the current narrative of sell-side commentary and investor commentary; how well known is each to investors who own or cover the company or its sector?

C58

Question asked How comprehensively are these dynamics articulated in the current narrative of (i) sell-side commentary and (ii) investor commentary (for example on Twitter): how well known is each of them to investors who own or cover the company or its sector, primarily for the next 18 months and secondarily for 18 months to three years out?

The judgement. Thinly, by few, and unevenly in a way that favours the investment case. The articulated narrative is Zeus Capital's, and it covers the headline dynamics well: defence demand, the NSPA comparator and a flat FY27, the order book, the valuation discount (about 15x forward against 18.2x for the sector when Zeus wrote it), the 2030 ambition, and the procurement-timing risk that the November 2024 cut taught everyone. The dynamics that decide the case in our assessment are the ones almost nobody has written down: the arithmetic of the fixed-cost leverage in both directions (about £3m of operating profit per £10m of Systems revenue; a £20m cushion of recurring revenue above break-even), the FY27 phasing and the probability-weighted second-half tranche, the through-life support tail and the Persistent Systems channel terms behind it, the working-capital and revenue-recognition disclosures, the withdrawal of the 12% margin target, the LTIP band, the balance sheet's move to net cash with no capital-allocation framework, the unhedged dollar purchasing, and the fact that the Defence Investment Plan is published and did not re-rate the stock. Investor commentary (bulletin boards, Twitter, fund manager notes) is not captured in the research at all, there is no expert-call coverage of the company anywhere in the pool, and the only visible investor act in the period is the chair's purchase of 12,500 shares at 159p in December 2025. The practical consequence for the November and December events is that the market knows what to expect of the H1 revenue line and does not know what to expect of the operating margin, the utilisation figure, the bridge, the programme share or the capital allocation; those are where surprise, in either direction, will come from.

Coverage, stated plainly. Covering houses: Zeus Capital (corporate broker through FY26, joint broker from 1 October 2026; active notes on 15 November 2024, 10 December 2024, 11 March 2025, 30 April 2025, 8 July 2025, 30 October 2025, 4 November 2025, 1 December 2025, 8 April 2026, 29 June 2026, 28 July 2026, 13 October 2026); Cavendish (nominated adviser and joint broker; the only contributor FactSet names, 8 September 2026, Buy, 301p; no standalone research in the pool); Berenberg (initiated 16 December 2025, Buy, reported via Sharecast and Investing.com; no notes in the pool); Allenby Capital and Shore Capital (historical, 2023). The research for this set covers the one house with documents, which is the whole of what exists to cover; the company's own three-house average is therefore known in full only to the company. Sector commentary that touches the stock by implication: Deutsche Bank and Jefferies on discoverIE, TT Electronics, XP Power, Volex and Cohort; Morgan Stanley on the Defence Investment Plan; Zeus's Industrials Monthly (July and October 2026) on UK defence small caps and takeovers.

The dynamics, one by one: who says it, since when, with what view, and how well known it is.

The NSPA non-repeat and the H1 FY27 optical decline: Zeus, since 1 December 2025 and explicitly on 29 June 2026, models a flat FY27 and says other divisions compensate; the company separates the one-off in every order-book figure. Well known to anyone who reads the broker; a revenue decline of about 12% in December will not surprise the informed and may surprise the screen.

Fixed-cost operating leverage, the £42m overhead and Ashchurch's £15m: Zeus (1 December 2025; 13 October 2026) names utilisation as the margin driver and calls the opportunity "material"; the company discloses the £3m overhead and £15m capacity in presentations. Known as a direction, not as a number; nobody has published the drop-through or the break-even, and the downside symmetry (unabsorbed overhead in a slip) is discussed only in the context of November 2024. Partly known.

The timing dependence on CAIN follow-ons and NSPA follow-ons: Zeus (29 June 2026) says follow-ons "could compensate" for the NSPA non-repeat and left forecasts unchanged on the CAIN award; the company says the initial order is a platform for follow-on revenues; the structure (16 Air Assault Brigade first, Land Environment Tactical Communication and Information Systems funding, the Segmented Acquisition Model's one-year lane) is in government and Persistent Systems documents, not in broker notes. Known as a hope; the tranche structure, the decision points and the three-to-six-month lag from the plan are discussed by no one covering the stock.

The through-life support tail: management (December 2025) and the April 2025 update describe "annuity streams"; Zeus mentions support and training; no one quantifies it, and the Persistent Systems precedents (WaRTAK, the Baltic network) appear only in this set's research. Little known.

The Persistent Systems exclusivity and sole-source dependence: disclosed in the annual report and in Persistent's own releases; "minimal attention in available broker commentary". Little known as a risk; known as a credential.

Power's autonomy orders and Volklec: Zeus (8 April 2026) highlights the $20m of orders and leaves forecasts unchanged "on a prudent basis"; the Volklec release (September 2025) is public. Known; not modelled, which is where the FY28 upside sits.

Components' 4.0% margin against the 7% to 9% target and the restock: Zeus and the company; the peer restock narrative (discoverIE +22% orders, XP Power book-to-bill 1.67) is the sector's loudest story and Solid State is not in it. Known; the structural reason the target is unlikely to be reached early (franchised distribution economics) is not discussed.

Memory inflation and programme viability: the company (29 June 2026 results; the chief executive's call) and Zeus (29 June 2026); Morgan Stanley and Deutsche Bank on the sector; the 2021 to 2023 precedent of margins held is not cited by anyone. Known as a risk; the company's demonstrated pass-through record is not known, which means the market may over-fear the margin and under-fear the timing.

Unhedged dollar purchasing (about 40%): disclosed with a sensitivity (£0.82m per 10%); "inadequately discussed" by the sell side. Little known.

The 17% customer: disclosed; Zeus notes it; nobody analyses its identity, term or recurrence (the FY24 20% customer). Partly known.

Working-capital quality (inventories £30.5m, work in progress up 147%, bill-and-hold judgement): in the audit committee's key audit matters; "virtually no commentary" in broker research. Unknown to the market; it is a risk only if it becomes an event, and it is one of the two dynamics (with the dollar) where a negative surprise would be wholly new.

The withdrawal of the 12% group margin target: visible by comparison of the May 2024 Capital Markets Day with the FY26 annual report; "not widely noted or challenged". Unknown; the Capital Markets Day will make it known one way or the other.

The LTIP band (11.5p to 16.0p for FY28): in the annual report; no sell-side comment. Unknown as a management expectation band, which it is.

The 2030 ambition (20p, £250m): Zeus and Berenberg cite it; the company repeats it. Well known as a headline; the bridge is known to no one, including, on the evidence, the company.

Net cash by March 2028 with no buyback or framework: Zeus models net cash; no one discusses capital allocation; Volex's £40m buyback is the peer contrast. Partly known; the option is not priced.

The succession and the chair: known from regulatory news; the chief executive's strategy "has not been fully presented" and the chair's procurement access is noted by Berenberg's initiation only. Known as fact, not as a thesis.

The Capital Markets Day: known as a date (announced 13 October 2026); its content is anticipated by Zeus as a margin story. Known.

Consolidation of accredited niches (discoverIE's Trival at over 30% EBIT margin and 3Gmetalworx at 11x to 12x EBITA, Diploma's CDM, Gooch & Housego at 25.9x and a 41% premium): widely discussed for the acquirers; applied to Solid State only in Zeus's July 2026 takeover note in general terms. Known at sector level; not articulated as a floor for this stock.

The Defence Investment Plan being published and the share falling afterwards: Morgan Stanley and Jefferies articulate the plan and its gap; no broker note on Solid State in the pool connects the two. Unknown as a reading, and important: the market has not been told that the clearing event has cleared nothing and that the next one is a contract.

The share-price fall with estimates unchanged: known to holders; attributed by Zeus to an unjustified discount, by the event work to timing fear and AIM flows. Known, without a cause attached.

How well known, for the two horizons. For the next 18 months the market knows the comparator, the order book, the defence tailwind, the valuation discount and the general procurement risk; it does not know the phasing arithmetic, the leverage arithmetic, the tranche probability, the pass-through record, the dollar, the working-capital disclosures or the published plan's non-effect. For 18 months to three years it knows the 2030 ambition and the divisional targets; it does not know the support tail, the Persistent channel economics, the United States execution requirement, the engineering-bench constraint, the capital-allocation option or the LTIP band. The asymmetry favours the case: the unknown positives (leverage to the upside, the support tail, the FY28 catch-up, net cash) are larger in value than the unknown negatives (the dollar, working capital), while the unknown negative that matters most (the FY27 phasing risk) is a timing risk the market has already partly priced by taking the multiple to the first percentile without knowing exactly why.

What the November and December events would make new, and what they would merely confirm. New: a utilisation figure or revenue capacity for Ashchurch; a dated margin bridge or the fate of the 12% figure; the programme and support share of Systems revenue; a capital-allocation framework; an H1 operating margin above or below 5.5%; any statement on the second-half tranche; a divisional order book. Confirming: a revenue decline of about 12%; an order book near £100m; the defence tailwind language; a Components margin near 4%; the 2030 ambition restated. The memo's topic #7 (scope for upgrades to the narrative) should rest on the first list.

Peers, for calibration of how a well-articulated narrative looks. discoverIE's restock story is carried by Deutsche Bank, Jefferies and Rothschild & Co Redburn with quarterly order data and a design-win bank, and the shares rose 29% in eleven weeks on it; Volex's data-centre and buyback story is carried by Jefferies and Zeus with monthly upgrades; XP Power's semiconductor orders are the sector's current talking point; TT Electronics' turnaround has three houses; Cohort and Chemring have Jefferies and Deutsche Bank with order-cover analysis. Solid State has one active house, no expert calls and no visible investor commentary, which is why its dynamics are under-articulated and why, if the Capital Markets Day and the interim supply the numbers, the re-articulation can be fast: there is no entrenched wrong narrative to displace, only an absent one.

Co-movement of SOLI-GB with its dashboard peers (computed 2026-10-22 from the master dashboard's daily prices)

What this is: daily close-to-close returns over rolling windows of 126 trading days (about six months). Correlation: how closely the daily moves line up (1 = always together, 0 = unrelated). Beta: how far the stock moves for a 1% move in the basket. R-squared: the share of the stock's daily price variance the peer basket explains; the remainder is the stock's own (idiosyncratic) movement. Basket: the peers below, equal-weighted. Prices only; the multiple's own history is in the financials block.

Appendix Research requests the Investment Analyst raised for the memo

Research requests for the next set

Company: Solid State plc (SOLI-GB, AIM, London), a UK value-added electronics group supplying defence and security, industrial, medical and transport markets through three divisions: Systems (Steatite ruggedised computing and secure tactical radio as the exclusive UK partner for Persistent Systems' MPU5 radios and Wave Relay networks, Active Silicon imaging, Q-Par antennas in the UK and United States, the Ashchurch integrated systems facility opened in FY26), Power (Custom Power custom battery packs in the UK and California, separated as a division in FY26) and Components (the Solsta specialist distribution and own-brand business: Pacer, Willow Technologies, Gateway, Durakool); fiscal year to 31 March.

Request: OWN-1

Question: Since the UK Ministry of Defence published its Defence Investment Plan on 30 June 2026 (which named Projects CAIN and EVE among its tactical radio network programmes), what contracts, contract notices, framework call-offs or procurement announcements have been issued under the Land Environment Tactical Communication and Information Systems initiative, under Project CAIN (for which Solid State plc's subsidiary Steatite received an initial $10.8m order on 30 October 2025, delivered in the quarter to June 2026), under the NATO Support and Procurement Agency communications framework from which Solid State plc delivered a £23.3m programme in H1 FY26, and under the Segmented Acquisition Model introduced in April 2026 for tactical communications upgrades; what has been published by the Ministry of Defence, HM Treasury, the National Armaments Director Group, Persistent Systems and its other channel partners, and the trade and defence press between July and October 2026 about the timing, funding line and tranche structure of the next Project CAIN phase and of any extension of MPU5 and Wave Relay fielding beyond 16 Air Assault Brigade and the Royal Marines; and what do the reports of October 2026 that the UK government may delay its spending review to autumn 2027 and slow the path to 3% of GDP, the £28bn gap between the Ministry's requirement and the Treasury's envelope reported by Jefferies on 17 May 2026, and the Competition and Markets Authority's September 2026 screening of Ministry of Defence procurement data imply for the conversion of that plan into contracts for sub-tier communications suppliers such as Solid State plc over the next twelve months?

Request: OWN-2

Question: What are the commercial terms, economics and durability of Solid State plc's subsidiary Steatite's exclusive UK partnership with Persistent Systems for MPU5 radios and Wave Relay networks (the channel margin on hardware, the split of hardware, software licence, training and through-life support value, the renewal date and territorial scope, and Persistent Systems' right or practice of selling or supporting directly to ministries), what revenue from training, support, spiral upgrades and software has followed the fielding of over 2,000 MPU5 radios by the Royal Marines under Project EVE (fully fielded by October 2025 according to Persistent Systems) and earlier UK deployments, and what do Persistent Systems' other international channel partners (DataHertz for the French Navy, the Baltic Sea coastline network partners under the $29m contracts announced on 10 March 2025, Rheinmetall Canada, and the WaRTAK network upgrades of $14.6m in 2020 and $7m in February 2026) and comparable tactical radio channel businesses (Codan Communications, the Silvus Technologies and Doodle Labs channel models) show about the timing, proportion of initial hardware value and margin of the support and upgrade revenue that follows initial deployments, and about the risk of the original equipment manufacturer bypassing the channel partner?

Request: OWN-3

Question: Who owns Solid State plc (SOLI-GB) and what is the current investor narrative about it: what do the shareholder register, regulatory holdings notifications, fund factsheets and fund manager commentary, UK small-cap investor bulletin boards, investor presentations (including the company's results webcasts and its Capital Markets Day announced for 25 November 2026) and social media say about the company since its FY26 results on 29 June 2026, which institutions and funds hold the shares and have added or reduced since the share price fell from about 199p in late June 2026 to about 162p by 22 October 2026 with broker forecasts unchanged, what the chair Victor Chavez's purchase of 12,500 shares in December 2025 and the directors' dealings announced in October 2026 show, how the register and investor commentary compare with those of discoverIE Group, TT Electronics, Volex, XP Power, Cohort, Chemring and Avon Technologies, and what investors in those peers and in Solid State plc say they are waiting for from the November 2026 Capital Markets Day and the H1 FY27 results on 15 December 2026?
